\displaystyle+2(g_{\mu\mu_{1}}q_{\nu}q_{\mu_{2}}\pm g_{\nu\mu_{1}}q_{\mu}q_{\mu_{2}})C_{5,6}^{2k}\Bigr]\times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right).

(A.66)

Here, the
C_{\iota=1,\dots,6}^{2k}
are scalar-valued coefficients that parameterize the normalization of each
c_{\mu\nu\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{2k}}^{\tau=2,\iota}
. The
C_{\iota}
are defined to all orders in QCD but can be identified and matched to quantities in fixed-order perturbation theory. The tensor part,
i.e., the part carrying Lorentz indices, can then be organized according to Lorentz structures as in Eq. (
A.46
).
Conventional factors of
2
in Eq. (
A
) are pulled from the
C_{\iota}^{2k}
in order to simplify later expressions.

Assembling these ingredients and contracting over all
2k>k_{\min}
indices, the OPE gives

\displaystyle\lim_{z\to 0}{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu}(q,p_{A})\ \overset{\rm OPE}{=}\

\displaystyle-2i\ \sum_{k=k_{\min}}^{\infty}\ \Bigl[-2g_{\mu\nu}q_{\mu_{1}}q_{\mu_{2}}C_{1}^{2k}+g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}Q^{2}C_{2}^{2k}-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}g^{\alpha}_{\mu_{1}}q^{\beta}q_{\mu_{2}}C_{3}^{2k}

\displaystyle+4\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{Q^{2}}q_{\mu_{1}}q_{\mu_{2}}C_{4}^{2k}+2(g_{\mu\mu_{1}}q_{\nu}q_{\mu_{2}}\pm g_{\nu\mu_{1}}q_{\mu}q_{\mu_{2}})C_{5,6}^{2k}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\times A_{\tau=2}^{2k}(p_{A}^{2})\times\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)

(A.67)

\displaystyle\equiv~

\displaystyle~\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}+\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}+\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}+\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4\mu\nu}+\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}+\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)

(A.68)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~-g_{\mu\nu}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1}+\frac{p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}}{M_{A}^{2}}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2}-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\frac{p^{\alpha}_{A}q^{\beta}}{M_{A}^{2}}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3}+\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{M_{A}^{2}}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4}

\displaystyle~+\frac{(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}+p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})}{M_{A}^{2}}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5}+\frac{(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}-p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})}{M_{A}^{2}}\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6}+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2).

(A.69)

The starting point for the summation over
k
depends on the particular Wilson coefficient;
specifically,

k_{\min}=2
for
C_{2}

while

k_{\min}=1
for the other
C_{i}
coefficients.
In Eq. (
A.69
), the scalar-valued coefficients
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i=1,\dots,6}^{A}
, i.e., the quantities
without
external Lorentz indices, are related to the hadronic structure functions
W_{i}^{A}
through the dispersion relationships of Eqs. (
A.45
)
and (
A.47
),
up to
(1/Q)
corrections.
The
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}
are given explicitly in terms of summations over
k
and
j
below in Eqs. (
A.96
)-(
A.106
).

In the intermediate step Eq. (
A.68
), each
\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota=1,\dots,6\ \mu\nu}^{A}
denotes the collection of contractions that are respectively proportional to the coefficient
C_{\iota}
.
We introduce this step because

\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
and
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}

do not have a
one-to-one correspondence when
M_{A}\neq 0
.
As a consequence of the normalizations adopted for
\tilde{W}_{i}^{A}
in Eq. (
A.31
),
and subsequently those for
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}
in Eq. (
A.46
),
some
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}
are
sourced by multiple
OPE operators when
M_{A}\neq 0
.
This phenomenon of
\tilde{W}_{i}^{A}
(or
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}
) being sourced by two or more
C_{\iota}
is sometimes called “structure function mixing,”
and is discussed in App.
A.4
.

We now derive all six
\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
from Eq. (
A
) by contracting all
\mu_{m}
indices.
After, we reorganize
\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
and group terms according to their Lorentz structure, e.g., collect all terms proportional to
g_{\mu\nu}
or all terms proportional to
\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}p^{\alpha}_{A}q^{\beta}
.
We then identify the
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}
,
which are in terms of hadronic matrix elements
A_{\tau=2}^{2k}
, Wilson coefficients
C_{\iota}^{2k}
, and nested summations.
In App.
A.5
, the summations are first evaluated for the case of a massless target.
The results for the
(M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})\neq 0
case
are then expressed in terms of structure functions for massless targets.

Organization of Lorentz contractions in the OPE

Explicit evaluation of all contractions of Lorentz indices in Eq. (
A
) is an exacting task. Therefore, as a first step, we organize everything according to

\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
, and hence
C_{\iota}
. As a consequence, patterns in
\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
appear and simplify the work.
The outcome is summarized in App.
A.4
.

(i)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}
:

We start with
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
because, like
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4\mu\nu}
, the covariant tensor structure for
\mu_{m=1}\dots\mu_{m=2k}
consists entirely of momentum factors
q
. That is to say, all lowered internal indices
\mu_{m}
are carried by
q_{\mu_{m}}
. This results in the simplest possible permutations of metrics and momenta in
\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}
. Combining terms, one gets

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-4i\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[-g_{\mu\nu}C_{1}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times\left(\prod_{m=1}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\times\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2),

(A.70)

where the production factor
(\prod_{m=1}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}})
has (re)absorbed
q_{\mu_{1}}
and
q_{\mu_{2}}
.

The contraction of
\prod_{m=1}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}
and the momentum factor
\tilde{\Pi}
combines all possible permutations of
2k
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
with
j
metrics
g^{\mu_{m}\mu_{n}}
in
\{g\dots g\}
and
(2k-2j)
momenta
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
in
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
. This results in
(2k-2j)
products of
(q\cdot p_{A})
. The remaining
(2k-0)-(2k-2j)=2j
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contract with the
2j
indices from
j
metrics. As no instance of
g^{\mu_{m}\mu_{n}}
or
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
have been pulled from
\tilde{\Pi}
, its permutation factor
\eta
is the same as in Eq. (
A.64
). The outcome is

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=1}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}(-1)^{j}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\ \frac{1}{2^{j}}\frac{(2k)!}{j!(2k-2j)!}\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\qquad(q^{2})^{j}(q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j)}(p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.71)

\displaystyle=\frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=0}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{j!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ ,

(A.72)

where
x_{A}=Q^{2}/(2p_{A}\cdot q)
is the Bjorken scaling variable and
p_{A}^{2}=M_{A}^{2}
.
Altogether,

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad-g_{\mu\nu}\ \times\ (-4i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{1}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{j!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2).

(A.73)

We stress that that the quantity to the right of
g_{\mu\nu}
here is not
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1}
in Eq. (
A.69
).
In general, the coefficients
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
receive contributions from more than one
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}
.

(ii)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4\mu\nu}
:

The next term we construct is
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4\mu\nu}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
, which has an identical tensor structure of contracted covariant indices as
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}
.
In other words, like
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}
, all the lowered internal indices
\mu_{m}
are carried exclusively by factors of
q_{\mu_{m}}
. The result is then,

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4\mu\nu}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{Q^{2}}\ \times\ (-8i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{4}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{j!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2).

(A.74)

(iii)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}
:

Moving onto
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
, exactly one covariant index from the collection
\mu_{m=1}\dots\mu_{m=2k}
is carried by the metric
g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}
. All other covariant instances of
\mu_{m}
are carried by
(2k-1)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
. This difference among the lowered indices introduces a complication that was absent in the previous cases but nevertheless allows us to write

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-2i\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\ q^{\beta}\ C_{3}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times g^{\alpha}_{\mu_{1}}\times\left(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\times\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.75)

In principle,
g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}
can contract with a metric from the collection
\{g\dots g\}
when it contains the index
\mu_{m=1}
.
This extracts a factor of
q^{\alpha}
out of the
production factor
(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}})
, and hence potential permutations,
through the chain
q^{\alpha}=g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}q_{\mu_{n}}g^{\mu_{n}\mu_{1}}
.
However, this generates a term proportional to
\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}q^{\beta}q^{\alpha}
, which is zero due to the antisymmetric tensor.

It is also possible for the metric
g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}
to contract with a momentum factor from the collection
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
. This extracts a factor of
p_{A}^{\alpha}=g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}p_{A}^{\mu_{1}}
, and reduces the possible number of permutations in the momentum factor
\tilde{\Pi}
. The contractions within
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}
then involve
(2k-1)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
contracting with
(2k-2j-1)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
, to make
(2k-2j-1)
powers of
(q\cdot p_{A})
. The remaining
(2k-1)-(2k-2j-1)=2j
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contract with the remaining
j
metrics.
Finally, since the number of available
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
has shifted (by one unit),
the multiplicity factor in Eq. (
A.64
) must be updated accordingly.
With the updated
\eta
factor, the outcome is

\displaystyle g_{\mu_{1}}^{\alpha}\left(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\quad=\quad

\displaystyle p_{A}^{\alpha}\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\ \frac{1}{2^{j}}\frac{(2k-1)!}{j!(2k-2j-1)!}\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ (q^{2})^{j}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j-1)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.76)

\displaystyle=\frac{2p_{A}^{\alpha}}{Q^{2}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\ .

(A.77)

Note that the summation over
j
extends only to
j=k-1
since there are fewer allowed permutations; this can be surmised from the denominator factor
1/(2k-2j-1)!
,
which would reduce to
1/(-1)!
were
j=k
.
After assembling the different terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}\quad

\displaystyle=\quad-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\frac{p_{A}^{\alpha}q^{\beta}}{Q^{2}}\ \times\ (-4i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{3}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\

\displaystyle\times\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\quad+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.78)

Unlike the other
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
in Eq. (
A.69
),
we can identify the quantity to the right of the antisymmetric tensor
\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
as
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3}
since there is a one-to-one correspondence.

(iv)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}
:

The steps to determine
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}
(and
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}
)
for
M_{A}\neq 0
closely follow those of
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3\mu\nu}
due to the presence of only one metric carrying a lowered index from the collection
\mu_{m=1}\dots\mu_{m=2k}
. The difference, however, is the absence of an antisymmetric tensor. The contractions of
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{1}}
with the collections
\{g\dots g\}
and
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
will thus generate two sets of terms each.
To obtain both sets of terms, we first write

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-4i\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[(g_{\mu\mu_{1}}q_{\nu}+g_{\nu\mu_{1}}q_{\mu})C_{5}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times\left(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\times\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.79)

For the case of the metric
g_{\alpha\mu_{1}}
contracting with
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
, we have from Eq. (
A.77
)

\displaystyle g_{\alpha\mu_{1}}\left(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|_{\mu_{1}\in\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}}=

\displaystyle\frac{2p_{A\alpha}}{Q^{2}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\ .

(A.80)

For the second case,
g_{\alpha\mu_{1}}
contracts with a metric in
\{g\dots g\}
and extracts a factor of
q_{\alpha}=g_{\alpha\mu_{1}}q_{\mu_{n}}g^{\mu_{n}\mu_{1}}
out of the production factor
(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}})
.
The precise factor of
q_{\mu_{n}}
that is extracted can be any one of the
(2k-1)
elements in the product factor, implying an additional multiplicity factor of this size
[
67
]
.
The remaining
(2k-2)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contract with
(2k-2j)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
to make
(2k-2j)
powers of
(q\cdot p_{A})
. The remaining
(2k-2)-(2k-2j)=2j-2
factors of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contract with the
2(j-1)
indices of the remaining
(j-1)
metrics. After updating
\eta
and noting that the sum now starts at
j=1
, the outcome is

\displaystyle g_{\alpha\mu_{1}}\left(\prod_{m=2}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|_{\mu_{1}\in\{g\dots g\}}=q_{\alpha}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}(-1)^{j}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\ \frac{1}{2^{j-1}}\frac{(2k-2)!}{(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \times(2k-1)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ (q^{2})^{(j-1)}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.81)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2q_{\alpha}}{Q^{2}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\

\displaystyle\frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ .

(A.82)

Assembling all terms for both
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{1}}
, we obtain the expression

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}+p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})}{Q^{2}}\

\displaystyle\quad\times\ (-8i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{5}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}

\displaystyle-\frac{(q_{\mu}q_{\nu}+q_{\nu}q_{\mu})}{Q^{2}}\

\displaystyle\quad\times\ (-8i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{5}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.83)

Importantly, there are two tensor structures:
The first is
(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}+p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})
, whose coefficient contributes to
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5}
in Eq. (
A.69
).
The second is
q_{\mu}q_{\nu}
, whose coefficient contributes to
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4}
in Eq. (
A.69
).
Note that the second term
can equally be written as
2(q_{\mu}q_{\nu})
; we do not (yet) condense the result in order to emphasize that
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{1}}
each generates two terms.

(v)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}
:

The next term,
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
, can be obtained directly from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}
by making the replacement
q_{\mu}\to-q_{\mu}
and
C_{5}\to C_{6}
. When making this replacement, the second part of Eq. (
A.83
) vanishes since
(q_{\mu}q_{\nu}+q_{\nu}q_{\mu})\to(q_{\mu}q_{\nu}-q_{\nu}q_{\mu})=0
.
The final result for
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}
is

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}-p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})}{Q^{2}}\ \times(-8i)\times\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{6}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\quad+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.84)

(vi)
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}
:

The final term
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
, which is given by

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=(-2i)\ \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left[Q^{2}C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \times\frac{2^{2k}}{(Q^{2})^{2k}}\times g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}\times\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\times\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.85)

is the most nuanced because the covariant tensor structure involves two metrics. That is to say, two lowered indices from the collection
\mu_{m=1}\dots\mu_{m=2k}
are carried by
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
. The contraction of these metrics with the collections
\{g\dots g\}
and
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
, in turn, generates five terms with four distinct Lorentz structures. The five possible ways are:

(a)

both
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
contract with momenta from
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
and extract
p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}
;

(b)

both
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
contract with
different
metrics from
\{g\dots g\}
and extract the quantity
q_{\mu}q_{\nu}
from the production factor
(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}})
;

(c)

both
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
contract with
the same
metric from
\{g\dots g\}
and extract
g_{\mu\nu}
;

(d)

g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
contracts with
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
and

g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
with
\{g\dots g\}
to extract
(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu})
;

(e)

same as (d) but with the index exchange
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
to extract the quantity
(p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})
.

In case (a), contracting the metrics with the collection
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
leaves
(2k-2j-2)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
to contract with
(2k-2)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
. The remaining
(2k-2)-(2k-2j-2)=2j
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contracts with the
2j
indices of the
j
metrics in the collection
\{g\dots g\}
. Updating the permutation factor, we get

\displaystyle g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}}=p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\ \frac{1}{2^{j}}\frac{(2k-2)!}{j!(2k-2j-2)!}\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ (-1)^{j}(q^{2})^{j}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j-2)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.86)

\displaystyle=\frac{4p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}}{Q^{4}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\

\displaystyle\frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-2)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-2)}\ .

(A.87)

Note that the summation over
j
extends only to
j=k-1
since there are fewer allowed permutations; this can be inferred from the denominator factor
1/(2k-2j-2)!
.

In case (b), contracting
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
with different metrics from the collection
\{g\dots g\}
leaves
(2k-4)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
to contract with
(2k-2j)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
.
The precise
q_{\mu_{m}}
that are extracted from the product factor
\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)
can be any two of its
(2k-2)
elements, implying the additional multiplicity factor
(2k-2)\times(2k-3)
.
The remaining
(2k-4)-(2k-2j)=(2j-4)
factors of
q_{\mu_{m}}
then contract with the
(j-2)
metrics remaining in
\{g\dots g\}
. Updating the
\eta
factor and noting the summation over
j
starts at
j=2
, we get

\displaystyle g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\Bigg|_{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\{g\dots g\}}

\displaystyle=q_{\mu}q_{\nu}\ \sum_{j=2}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\ \frac{1}{2^{j-2}}\frac{(2k-4)!}{(j-2)!(2k-2j)!}\ \times\ (2k-3)(2k-2)\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ (-1)^{j}(q^{2})^{(j-2)}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.88)

\displaystyle=\frac{4q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{Q^{4}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=2}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-2)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ .

(A.89)

In case (c), contracting
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
and
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
with the same metric from the collection
\{g\dots g\}
leaves
(2k-2)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
to contract with all
(2k-2j)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
. The
(2k-2)-(2k-2j)=(2j-2)
uncontracted instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
are then matched with the
(j-1)
metrics that are left in
\{g\dots g\}
. Updating
\eta
and the summation over
j
, which now starts from
j=1
, we get

\displaystyle g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}\

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\Bigg|_{g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\in\{g\dots g\}}\ =\ g_{\mu\nu}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \frac{1}{2^{j-1}}\frac{(2k-2)!}{(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ (-1)^{j}(q^{2})^{(j-1)}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.90)

\displaystyle=\ -\frac{2g_{\mu\nu}}{Q^{2}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ .

(A.91)

In case (d),
g_{\mu\mu_{1}}
contracts with
\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}
, leaving
(2k-2j-1)
instances of
p_{A}^{\mu_{m}}
, while
g_{\nu\mu_{2}}
contracts with
\{g\dots g\}
, and eventually
(\prod_{\mu=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{n}})
, leaving a total of
(2k-3)
instances of
q_{\mu_{m}}
. The result is
(2k-2j-1)
powers of
(q\cdot p_{A})
and
(2k-3)-(2k-2j-1)=(2j-2)
factors of
q_{\mu_{m}}
to contract with
(j-1)
metrics.
The
q_{\mu_{m}}
that is extracted from the product factor can be any one of its initial
(2k-2)
elements, implying an additional multiplicity factor of this size.
Updating
\eta
and noting the summation over
j
runs from
j=1
to
j=k-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle g_{\mu\mu_{1}}g_{\nu\mu_{2}}\left(\prod_{m=3}^{2k}q_{\mu_{m}}\right)\tilde{\Pi}^{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{2k}}\Bigg|^{\mu_{1}\in\{p_{A}\dots p_{A}\}}_{\mu_{2}\in\{g\dots g\}}=p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!}{2^{j}(2k)!}\

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \frac{1}{2^{j-1}}\frac{(2k-3)!}{(j-1)!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \times(2k-2)\ (-1)^{j}(q^{2})^{(j-1)}\ (q\cdot p_{A})^{(2k-2j-1)}\ (p_{A}^{2})^{j}

(A.92)

\displaystyle=-\frac{4p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}}{Q^{4}}\ \frac{(Q^{2})^{2k}}{2^{2k}}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\ .

(A.93)

Case (e) follows from case (d) but with
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
exchanged.

After assembling all five cases and regrouping, we find

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{2p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}}{Q^{2}}\ \times(-4i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\

\displaystyle\quad~\times\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-2)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-2)}

\displaystyle-g_{\mu\nu}\

\displaystyle\times(-4i)\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{Q^{2}}\

\displaystyle\times(-4i)\ \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=2}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-2)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(p_{A\mu}q_{\nu}+p_{A\nu}q_{\mu})}{Q^{2}}\ \times(-4i)\ \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\

\displaystyle\quad~\times\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.94)

The result implies that
\Delta\tilde{T}_{2\mu\nu}^{A}
, and hence the Wilson coefficient
C_{2}^{2k}
, contributes to
\Delta T_{1}^{A}
,
\Delta T_{2}^{A}
,
\Delta T_{4}^{A}
, and
\Delta T_{5}^{A}
in Eq. (
A.69
). The absence of contributions to
\Delta T_{3}^{A}
and
\Delta T_{6}^{A}
, which are asymmetric under
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
exchange, can be traced to the tensor coefficient multiplying
C_{2}^{2k}
in
c_{\mu\nu\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{2k}}^{\tau=2,\iota}
, which is symmetric under
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
exchange.

A.4  Nuclear structure functions from the OPE II:
structure-function mixing

Using the expressions for
\Delta\tilde{T}_{\iota\ \mu\nu}^{A}
, we finally obtain the quantities
\Delta\tilde{T}_{i}^{A}
in Eq. (
A.69
). For
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1}
and
M_{A}\neq 0
, we collect like-terms from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1\mu\nu}
and
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}
to obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1}=(-4i)\

\displaystyle\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{1}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle+(-4i)\

\displaystyle\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.95)

\displaystyle=(-4i)\

\displaystyle\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\ \quad\ \times\ \left[\left(C_{1}^{2l+2j+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+2j+1}\right)+\frac{j\left(C_{2}^{2l+2j+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+2j+1}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\right]\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.96)

To reach Eq. (
A.96
), a factor of
1=(2k)(2k-1)j/(2k)(2k-1)j
is introduced into the
C_{2}
term to complete the factorials.
The summation in the
C_{2}
term can then be extended to include
j=0
since the contribution is zero.
We also re-index the summation over
k
into a summation over
2l=2k-2j-1
. After this shift it becomes manifest that the contribution from the
C_{2}
Wilson coefficient vanishes when the summation over
j
is truncated at
j=0
. It also becomes clear that structure-function mixing is due to TMCs since neglecting terms where
j>0
is equivalent to taking the
(M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})\to 0
limit.

The coefficient
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2}
is sourced entirely from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu 2}
, and is given by

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-2)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-2)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-2)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.97)

\displaystyle=(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}(2l+1)(2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.98)

In reaching Eq. (
A.98
) we again completed the factorials and set
2l=2k-2j-1
.

Similarly,
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3}
for
M_{A}\neq 0
is sourced entirely from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu 3}
. It is given by

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3}=

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{3}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.99)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j)!}{j!(2l)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ (2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{3}^{(2l+2j)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j)}\right)}{(2l+2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.100)

where the index
l=k-j
was used after completing the factorials.

For
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4}
and
M_{A}\neq 0
, we collect terms from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu 4}
,
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu 2}
, and
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{\mu\nu 6}
. The result is

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4}\quad=(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{4}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\ .

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=2}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!{(2k-2)!}}{(2k)!(j-2)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{4M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{5}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=1}^{k}\ \frac{(2k-j)!{(2k-1)!}}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j)}\

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.101)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\times\left[\left(C_{4}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)+\ \frac{j\ (j-1)\ \left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}-\ \frac{2j\ \left(C_{5}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)}\ \right]\

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.102)

We complete the factorials using factors of
1=(2k)j/(2j)j
, etc., and use

2l=2k-2j-1
to re-index the summation over
k
. The summations over
j
can also be extended to
j=0
since the additional terms are proportional to
j
or
j(j-1)
, i.e., are zero at
j=0
and/or
j=1
, and hence do not contribute.

The coefficient
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5}
for
M_{A}\neq 0

is generated by
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2\mu\nu}

and
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5\mu\nu}
. The result is

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5}

\displaystyle=(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{5}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}

\displaystyle-(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\left[C_{2}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!{(2k-2)!}}{(2k)!(j-1)!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.103)

\displaystyle=(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}x_{A}^{-2l}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\times\left[\frac{(2l+1)\ \left(C_{5}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)}\ -\frac{j\ (2l+1)\ \left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\ \right]

\displaystyle\ +\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.104)

where the index reassignment is
2l=2k-2j-1
.

Finally, the coefficient
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6}

is generated solely by
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6\mu\nu}
. It is given by

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6}=

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\ \left[C_{6}^{2k}A_{\tau=2}^{2k}\right]\ \sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ \frac{(2k-j)!(2k-1)!}{(2k)!j!(2k-2j-1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ x_{A}^{-(2k-2j-1)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.105)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-4i)\ \frac{2M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\ \sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j)!}{j!(2l)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ (2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{6}^{(2l+2j)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j)}\right)}{(2l+2j)}\

\displaystyle+\quad\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.106)

where the index
l=k-j
was used after completing the factorials.

We briefly stress that the
(M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})
prefactor in all
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
is conventional. They exist to match the normalization of
\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
in Eq. (
A.69
) and structure functions
\tilde{W}^{A}_{i}
in Eq. (
A.31
). The massless target limit should be understood as truncating the summation over
j
at
j=0
. In addition, the re-indexing from
k
to
l
in the above expressions is not random. Different choices of relabeling can be found in the literature and depend on the precise interpretation of the quantities to the right of the
x_{A}^{-N}
factor.
Our choices are motivated by the attempt to align the ratio of factorials into binomial distributions, e.g.,
\binom{k+j}{j}=(k+j)!/j!k!
.

A.5  Nuclear structure fns. from the OPE III: massless and massive targets

Massless nuclear targets

As a final preliminary step to obtaining TMCs at leading twist, we
establish the connection between the effective Wilson coefficients
C_{\iota}
, the reduced matrix element
A_{\tau=2}
, and structure functions in the limit
that
(M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})\to 0
; in this limit, the TMCs will not be present, and we label this result as “No TMC” in Eq. (
A.107
)).
For each
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
above, truncating terms with
j>0
and using
the Taylor expansion of
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
from Eq. (
A.61
) gives the following:

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{1}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}\left(C_{1}^{2l+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+1}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l+1}\tilde{F}_{1}^{A(2l+1)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC},

(A.107a)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{2x_{A}M_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-2l}\ \left(C_{2}^{2l+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+1}\right)\ +\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l}\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A(2l)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC},

(A.107b)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{x_{A}M_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{3}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-2l}\ \left(C_{3}^{2l}A_{\tau=2}^{2l}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l}\ \tilde{F}_{3}^{A(2l)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC},

(A.107c)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{2M_{A}^{2}}\right)\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{4}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}\left(C_{4}^{2l+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+1}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l+1}\tilde{F}_{4}^{A(2l+1)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC},

(A.107d)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{2x_{A}M_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{5}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}\left(C_{5}^{2l+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+1}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l+1}\tilde{F}_{5}^{A(2l+1)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC},

(A.107e)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{Q^{2}}{2x_{A}M_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{6}\Bigg|_{j=0}

\displaystyle=-4i\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ x_{A}^{-2l}\ \left(C_{6}^{2l}A_{\tau=2}^{2l}\right)\ +\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\ \omega_{A}^{2l}\ \tilde{F}_{6}^{A(2l)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC}\ .

(A.107f)

The conclusion is that, in the massless target limit, one can identify the leading power of the product of the
N^{\rm th}

Wilson coefficient
C_{i}^{N}
(note the index shift from
\iota
to
i
) and hadronic matrix element
A_{\tau=2}^{N}

as a Mellin moment of structure functions themselves.
This is summarized by

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{i}^{AN}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC}

\displaystyle=C_{i}^{N}A_{\tau=2}^{N}\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,3-6\ ,

(A.108a)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A(N-1)}\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC}

\displaystyle=C_{2}^{N}A_{\tau=2}^{N}\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.108b)

As a technical remark, the normalization of Eq. (
A.108
) fixes the normalizations of
C_{i}
in Eqs. (
A
) and (
A
).

Massive nuclear targets

Given the relationship between the moments of
\tilde{F}_{i}^{A}\big|_{\rm No~TMC}
and the product
(C_{i}A_{\tau=2})
in the massless target limit, we are now in position to bootstrap expressions for
\tilde{F}_{i}^{A}
with target mass corrections.
The key to this is comparing the expansion of
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
in Eq. (
A.61
) with the full expressions
for
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
in
Eqs. (
A.96
)-(
A.106
).
One can identify the moments of structure functions with TMCs as everything encapsulated by the summation over
j
,
i.e., the coefficients multiplying the
x_{A}^{-2l(\pm 1)}
factor.
Using Eq. (
A.108
), the unknown/non-perturbative
(C_{i}A_{\tau=2})
can then be replaced by the measurable
\tilde{F}_{i}^{A}\big|_{\rm No~TMC}
.
Expressions in
x_{A}
-space are obtained by inverse Mellin transformations.
The transformations and summations over
j
are solvable using various identities.

\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}
:

To build
\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}
for a non-zero target mass, we start from
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{2}
in Eq. (
A.98
).
Using Eq. (
A.61
) (or Eq. (
A.107
)),
we identify everything to the right of
x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}
in Eq. (
A.98
) as the
(2l)^{th}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
with TMCs. This allows us to write

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A(2l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ (2l+1)(2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\ .

(A.109)

For an arbitrary integrable function
B(y)
over
y\in[0,1]
and
m\geq 0
, we have the identities

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(m+1)}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{m+1}\ B(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{m}\ H(y),\

(A.110a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(m+2)(m+1)}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{(m+2)}\ B(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{m}\ G(y),\

(A.110b)

\displaystyle\ \text{where}\qquad\qquad H(y)

\displaystyle=\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\ B(y^{\prime}),\

(A.110c)

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\qquad G(y)=\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\ \int_{y^{\prime}}^{1}dy^{\prime\prime}\ B(y^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\ (y^{\prime}-y)\ B(y^{\prime})\

(A.110d)

These identities will be used repeatedly for the different structure functions.
Schematically, the identities show that each factor of “
1/m
,” which originates from completing the (numerator) factorials in
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
, corresponds to an additional integral over a structure function.
Using the second identity
and Eq. (
A.108
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l+2j+1}\cdot y^{-2}\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(y)\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}

(A.111)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l+2j-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y),\quad\text{where}

(A.112)

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{2}(y)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\int_{y^{\prime}}^{1}dy^{\prime\prime}\ (y^{\prime\prime})^{-2}\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(y^{\prime\prime})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.113)

This allows allows us to evaluate the summation over
j
:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A(2l)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)\ (2l+1)(2l)\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!(2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{y^{2}M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

(A.114)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)\ \frac{(2l+1)(2l)\ y^{2l}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+2}}\ ,

(A.115)

where we used the first of the following identities, which also hold for
z>1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(n+j)!}{j!\ n!}\ z^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-z)^{(n+1)}}\ ,

(A.116a)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\ j\ \frac{(n+j)!}{j!\ n!}\ z^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(n+1)\ z}{(1-z)^{(n+2)}}\ ,

(A.116b)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\ j\ (j-1)\ \frac{(n+j)!}{j!\ n!}\ z^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(n+1)\ (n+2)\ z^{2}}{(1-z)^{(n+3)}}\ .

(A.116c)

The first identity is derived by Taylor expanding the right-hand side and completing the factorial.
The second follows from taking the derivative of the first identity and multiplying by
z
; similarly, the third by taking two derivatives and scaling by
z^{2}
.

After relabeling
N=2l
, and using the second of the following identities

\displaystyle(n+a)z^{-n}

\displaystyle=-z^{(1+a)}\ \frac{d}{dz}\ z^{-(n+a)}\ ,

(A.117a)

\displaystyle(n+a)(n+a+1)z^{-n}

\displaystyle=\ z^{(2+a)}\frac{d^{2}}{dz^{2}}\ z^{-(n+a)}\ ,

(A.117b)

we can take the inverse Mellin transformation of
\tilde{F}_{2}^{AN}
.
We remark that these derivative identities will be used repeatedly for the different structure functions. Schematically, they show that each factor of “
n
” or “
(n+1)
,” which originates from completing the factorials in
\Delta\tilde{T}^{A}_{i}
, corresponds to a derivative over the generating functions
G(y)
and
H(y)
.

Inserting the derivative identity and taking the inverse Mellin transformation gives

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ x_{A}^{-N}\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{AN}

(A.118)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}^{2}}{2\pi i}\frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\

(A.119)

\displaystyle=x_{A}^{2}\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \delta\left[\log\left(\frac{(y/x_{A})}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right)\right]\ ,

(A.120)

where we employed the contour integral

\displaystyle\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ z^{N}=(2\pi i)\ \delta\left[\log(z)\right]\ .

(A.121)

To evaluate the
\delta
function, we use the decomposition formula

\displaystyle\delta\left[g(z)\right]=\sum_{z_{k}}\ \frac{1}{|g^{\prime}(z)|}\ \delta(z-z_{k}),\quad\text{such~that}\quad g(z=z_{k})=0.

(A.122)

This allows us to express the
\delta
function in
\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A})
in terms of the zeros of the logarithm, i.e., when
(y/x)=(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})
. While the logarithm vanishes for two values of
y
, only one is physical. The unphysical solution
y_{2}
lies outside the domain
y\in[0,1]
, meaning the contribution from
\delta(y-y_{2})
is always zero.
The physical solution is the Nachtmann scaling variable
[
78
]
:

\displaystyle\xi_{A}=\frac{2x_{A}}{1+r_{A}}\ ,\quad\text{where}\quad r_{A}\equiv\sqrt{1+4x_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2}}=\frac{(1+\xi_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}{(1-\xi_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ ,

(A.123)

and corresponds to the following solution for the
\delta
function:

\displaystyle\delta\left[\log\left(\frac{(y/x_{A})}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right)\right]\ =\ \left[\frac{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}{(1+y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right]\ \delta(\xi_{A}-y)\ .

(A.124)

Some identities that are useful for mapping between results in the literature include

\displaystyle\frac{x_{A}}{r_{A}\xi_{A}}=\frac{1}{(1+\xi_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}}=\frac{1}{(1-\xi_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ .

(A.125)

Replacing the
\delta
function with Eq. (
A.124
) allows us to finally write

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=x_{A}^{2}\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \left[\frac{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}{(1+y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right]\ \delta(\xi-y)\

(A.126)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\ x_{A}^{2}\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\ \left[\frac{(1+r_{A})^{2}}{4r_{A}}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\right]\ \ .

(A.127)

Allowing the derivative to act on the quantities inside the bracket recovers Eq. (
3.24b
). The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
).
We omit intermediate steps, which is a tedious but straightforward
exercise in (one dimensional) differentiation
when
r_{A}
and
\xi_{A}
are treated as functions of
x_{A}
.
After successive applications of the product rule, i.e.,

(u\cdot v)^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\cdot v+u\cdot v^{\prime}
,
and the chain rule, i.e.,

(g\circ\xi\circ x)^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}\circ\xi)\cdot\xi^{\prime}(x)
,
the
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{g}_{2})
term in
Eqs. (
3.24b
) and (
A.165
)
is generated when both derivatives act on the
(1+r_{A})^{2}/4r_{A}
factor.
Likewise,
the
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{h}_{2})
term is generated when only one derivative acts on
\tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})
,
and
the
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{F}^{A}_{2})
term is generated when both derivatives act on
\tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})
.

Some identities that are helpful to obtain the final result include:

\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}
:

To build
\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}
, we similarly use Eq. (
A.61
) and identify everything to the right of
x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}

in Eq. (
A.100
) as the
2l^{th}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
with TMCs.
We then write

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A(2l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j)!}{j!(2l)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ (2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{3}^{(2l+2j)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j)}\right)}{(2l+2j)}\ .

(A.129)

Using the integral identity of Eq. (
A.110a
) and the relationship in Eq. (
A.108
) between the moment of
F_{3}^{A}
and the product
(C_{3}A_{\tau=2})
when
(M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{C_{3}^{(2l+2j)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j)}}{(2l+2j)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2l+2j)}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l+2j}\cdot y^{-1}\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(y)\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\

(A.130)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l+2j-1}\ \tilde{h}_{3}(y),\quad\text{where}

(A.131)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{3}(y)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\ (y^{\prime})^{-1}\ \tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(y^{\prime})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.132)

Using this, the summation over
j
is then given by

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A(2l)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{2l-1}\ \tilde{h}_{3}(y)\ (2l)\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j)!}{j!(2l)!}\ \left(\frac{y^{2}M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

(A.133)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ y^{-1}\ \tilde{h}_{3}(y)\ \frac{(2l)\ y^{2l}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\ .

(A.134)

After relabeling
N=2l
, using the first derivative identity in Eq. (
A.117
),
the contour integral of Eq. (
A.121
),
the
\delta
function decomposition of Eq. (
A.124
),
and the identities for
\xi_{A}
,
we get as the inverse Mellin transformation of
\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ x_{A}^{-N}\ \tilde{F}_{3}^{AN}

(A.135)

\displaystyle=\frac{-x_{A}}{2\pi i}\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{h}_{3}(y)}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\

(A.136)

\displaystyle=-x_{A}\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{h}_{3}(y)}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \left[\frac{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}{(1+y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right]\ \delta(\xi_{A}-y)\ ,

(A.137)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\ -x_{A}\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\ \tilde{h}_{3}(\xi_{A})\right]\ \ .

(A.138)

Allowing the derivative to act on the quantities inside the bracket recovers Eq. (
3.24c
). The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
).
In addition to Eq. (
A.128
), helpful identities
include:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left(\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(r_{A}^{2}-1)}{2x_{A}r_{A}^{3}}\ \quad\text{and}\quad\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \tilde{h}_{3}(\xi_{A})=-\left(\frac{d\xi_{A}}{dx_{A}}\right)\ \xi_{A}^{-1}\ \tilde{F}^{A}_{3}(\xi_{A})\Big|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.139)

\tilde{F}_{6}^{A}
:

Building
\tilde{F}_{6}^{A}
with TMCs proceeds identically to
\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}
.
We first identify everything to the right of
x_{A}^{-(2l-1)}
in Eq. (
A.106
) as the
2l^{th}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{6}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
. Explicitly,

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A(2l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j)!}{j!(2l)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ (2l)\ \frac{\left(C_{6}^{(2l+2j)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j)}\right)}{(2l+2j)}\ .

(A.140)

The procedure for this case is similar to
\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}
. Using the relationship in Eq. (
A.108
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{6}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=-x_{A}\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\ \tilde{h}_{6}(y)\right]\ ,\quad\text{where}\quad\tilde{h}_{6}(y)\equiv\int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}y^{\prime-1}\ \tilde{F}_{6}^{A}(y^{\prime})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.141)

The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
)
and can be obtained by using identities analogous to Eq. (
A.139
).
To our knowledge, the TMCs to the
\tilde{F_{6}}
structure function have not been previously reported.

\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}
:

In principle, building
\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}
follows the same procedure as the previous cases. However, the nuance here is that there are contributions from
(C_{1}A_{\tau=2})
and
(C_{2}A_{\tau=2})
when
j>0
. The solution is to decompose these into the moments of
\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}
and
\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}
, respectively.
Using Eqs. (
A.61
) and (
A.107
), we identify
everything to the right of
x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}

in Eq. (
A.96
) as the
(2l+1)^{st}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
with TMCs.
Symbolically, this is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{1}^{A(2l+1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\ \frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ \left[\left(C_{1}^{2l+2j+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+2j+1}\right)+\frac{j\left(C_{2}^{2l+2j+1}A_{\tau=2}^{2l+2j+1}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\right]\

(A.142)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\left[y^{2l+2j}\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(y)\Big|_{\rm No~TMCs}+j\ y^{2l+2j-1}\tilde{g}_{2}(y)\right],

(A.143)

where we expressed
(C_{2}A_{\tau=2})
as an integral over
\tilde{g}_{2}(y)
according to Eq. (
A.111
). Using Eq. (
A.116
), we evaluate the summation of each term separately, giving

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{1}^{A(2l+1)}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ }{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \left[\frac{y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\right]

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \left[\frac{(2l+2)y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\right].

(A.144)

We now set
N=(2l+1)
, take the inverse Mellin transformation with respect to
N
, and use the first derivative identity in Eq. (
A.117
) to remove the
(N+1)
. The result is

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(x_{A})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ x_{A}^{-N}\ \tilde{F}_{1}^{AN}

(A.145)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ }{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{2\pi iQ^{2}}\right)\

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}\ x_{A}^{-1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\right]

(A.146)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ }{(1+y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right]_{y=\xi_{A}}\

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)\ x_{A}^{-1}\ y}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})(1+y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\right]_{y=\xi_{A}}\ .

(A.147)

To reach the last line, we used the contour integral of Eq. (
A.121
) and the
\delta
function decomposition of Eq. (
A.124
).
Using the
\xi_{A}
identities, the final expressions is

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(x_{A})=

\displaystyle\frac{x_{A}}{r_{A}\xi_{A}}\ \tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ -\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\right]\quad.

(A.148)

Applying the derivative recovers Eq. (
3.24a
).
Helpful identities are given in Eqs. (
A.128
) and (
A.139
).
Note that the factor of
1/2
in the square brackets is absent in Eq. (14) of Ref.
[
73
]
.
This appears to be a typo in Ref.
[
73
]
as the final result
matches Eq. (
3.24a
).
Furthermore, after employing
\xi_{A}
identities, we find agreement with Eq. (3.1) of Ref.
[
67
]
.
The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
).

\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}
:

Building
\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}
with TMCs, which involves mixing with
\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}
, is similar to
\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}
. Subsequently, we identify everything to the right of
x_{A}^{-(2l)}
in Eq. (
A.104
) as the
(2l+1)^{th}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{5}^{A(2l+1)}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(2l+1)\ \left(C_{5}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)}-\frac{j\ (2l+1)\ \left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)(2l+2j)}\ \right]+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.149)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\left[\frac{(2l+1)}{2}\ y^{2l+2j}\ \tilde{h}_{5}(y)-j(2l+1)\ y^{2l+2j-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)\right]\

\displaystyle\qquad+\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2),\

(A.150)

where we used Eqs. (
A.108
) and (
A.111
) to decompose both
(C_{i}A_{\tau=2})
products as integrals over

\tilde{F}_{i}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMCs}
,
and have defined via the identity Eq. (
A.110a
)
the generating integral

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{5}(y)\

\displaystyle\equiv\ \int_{y}^{1}dy^{\prime}\ (y^{\prime})^{-1}\ 2\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(y^{\prime})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.151)

Note the factor of
2
in the definition of
\tilde{h}_{5}
, which follows the convention of Eq. (3.15) in Ref.
[
67
]
.

Using Eq. (
A.116
), we evaluate the summation of each term separately. This gives

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{5}^{A(2l+1)}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{h}_{5}(y)}{2y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \left[\frac{(2l+1)\ y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{(2l+1)}}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \left[\frac{(2l+2)\ (2l+1)\ y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{(2l+1)}}\right]\ +\mathcal{O}(\tau>2).\

(A.152)

Setting
N=(2l+1)
, taking the inverse Mellin transform with respect to
x_{A}
, and using the derivative identities of Eq. (
A.117
) allows us to obtain
\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(x_{A})
. It is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ x_{A}^{-N}\ \tilde{F}_{5}^{A(N)}

(A.153)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-x_{A}}{4\pi i}\right)\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{h}_{5}(y)}{y\ (1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{x_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}}{2\pi iQ^{2}}\right)\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2).\

(A.154)

Applying the contour integral of Eq. (
A.121
)
and the identity of Eq. (
A.124
), we get

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-x_{A}}{2}\right)\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\ \tilde{h}_{5}(\xi_{A})\right]\ -\left(\frac{x_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\left[\frac{(1+r_{A})^{2}}{4r_{A}}\ \xi_{A}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\right]\

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.155)

After accounting for the difference between
\tilde{W}_{5}^{A}
and
\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}
, this agrees with Eq. (3.5) of Ref.
[
67
]
.
The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
).
In addition to Eqs. (
A.128
) and (
A.139
), a helpful identity is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \tilde{h}_{5}(\xi_{A})=-2\left(\frac{d\xi_{A}}{dx_{A}}\right)\ \xi_{A}^{-1}\ \tilde{F}^{A}_{5}(\xi_{A})\Big|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ .

(A.156)

\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}
:

Building
\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}
follows the same procedure as above with the complication that the result is sourced by three contributions for
j>0
.
Using Eqs. (
A.61
) and (
A.107
), we identify
everything to the right of of
x_{A}^{-(2l+1)}
in Eq. (
A.102
) as the
(2l+1)^{st}
moment of
\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(x_{A},Q^{2})
. This is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{A(2l+1)}_{4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\times\left[\left(C_{4}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)+\frac{j(j-1)\left(C_{2}^{(2l+2j+!)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)\ (2l+2j)}-\frac{2j\left(C_{5}^{(2l+2j+1)}A_{\tau=2}^{(2l+2j+1)}\right)}{(2l+2j+1)}\right]\

\displaystyle\qquad+\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2),\

(A.157)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[y^{2l+2j}\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMC}\ +\ j(j-1)y^{2l+2j-1}\tilde{g}_{2}(y)\ -\ jy^{2l+2j}\tilde{h}_{5}(y)\right]\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.158)

Here, we again used the Eqs. (
A.108
), (
A.111
),
and
(
A.151
)
to rewrite
(C_{i}A_{\tau=2})
.

Distributing the summations and using the identities listed in Eq. (
A.116
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{A(2l+1)}_{4}=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \Bigg[y^{2l}\ \tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMC}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}y^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\

\displaystyle+\ y^{2l-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}j(j-1)\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}y^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle-\ y^{2l}\ \tilde{h}_{5}(y)\

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}j\frac{(2l+j+1)!}{j!\ (2l+1)!}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}y^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{j}\ \Bigg]+\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\

(A.159)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \Bigg[\frac{\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMC}}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\

\displaystyle\left[\frac{y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\right]\

\displaystyle+\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{2}\ \frac{y^{2}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{3}}\

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(2l+2)(2l+3)y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\right]\

\displaystyle-\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \frac{y\ \tilde{h}_{5}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{(2l+2)y^{2l+1}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2l+1}}\right]\ \Bigg]\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.160)

Relabeling
N=(2l+1)
and using the derivative identities in Eq. (
A.117
) gives

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(x_{A})=\frac{1}{2\pi i}

\displaystyle\int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ x_{A}^{-N}\ \tilde{F}_{4}^{AN}

(A.161)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(y)|_{\rm No~TMC}}{y(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\ \right]

\displaystyle+\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{2}\ \left(\frac{x_{A}^{3}}{2\pi i}\right)\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{y^{2}\ x_{A}^{-1}\ \tilde{g}_{2}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{3}}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\ \right]

\displaystyle+(-1)^{2}\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ \left(\frac{x_{A}^{2}}{2\pi i}\right)\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dy\ \frac{y\ x_{A}^{-1}\ \tilde{h}_{5}(y)}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{2}}\ \int_{c-i\infty}^{c+i\infty}dN\ \left[\frac{(y/x_{A})^{N}}{(1-y^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2})^{N}}\ \right]

\displaystyle+\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.162)

Finally, using contour integral of Eq. (
A.121
) and the
\xi_{A}
identities, we get

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(x_{A})=

\displaystyle\frac{(1+r_{A})}{2r_{A}}\ \tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMC}+\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)^{2}\ x_{A}^{3}\ \frac{d^{2}}{dx_{A}^{2}}\ \left[\left(\frac{x_{A}^{2}}{r_{A}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\right]

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\right)\ x_{A}^{2}\ \frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \left[\left(\frac{x_{A}}{r_{A}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{5}(\xi_{A})\right]\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.163)

After accounting for differences between
\tilde{W}_{4}^{A}
and
\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}
, this agrees with Eq. (3.4) of Ref.
[
67
]
.
The final expression is summarized in Eq. (
A.165
).
In addition to Eqs. (
A.128
),
(
A.139
),
and (
A.156
), a helpful identity is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{A}}\ \frac{x_{A}^{n}}{r_{A}}=\frac{(r_{A}^{2}+n-1)x_{A}^{n-1}}{r_{A}}\ .

(A.164)

Summary

We now give the full expressions for all six structure functions with TMCs. These are obtained by successive applications of the product and chain rules to the several expressions above. The final results are:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}}\ \tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.165a)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}^{2}}{\xi_{A}^{2}r_{A}^{3}}\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{6M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{4}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \left(\frac{12M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{4}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{5}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.165b)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}^{2}}\ \tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{3}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.165c)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}}\ \tilde{F}_{4}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ -\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{5}^{A}(\xi_{A})\ -\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{4}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{4}}\right)\ \left(2-\xi_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{5}}\right)\ \left(1-2x_{A}^{2}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.165d)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}^{2}}\ \tilde{F}_{5}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ -\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{3}\xi_{A}}\right)\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{5}^{A}(\xi_{A})\ -\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{4}}\right)\ \left(1-x_{A}\xi_{A}M_{A}^{2}/Q^{2}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \left(\frac{6M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{5}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.165e)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{6}^{A}(x_{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}^{2}}\ \tilde{F}_{6}^{A}(\xi_{A})\Bigg|_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{6}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.165f)

Importantly, the above expressions for
nuclear
structure functions
\tilde{F}_{1},\dots,\tilde{F}_{5}
agree with those for
nucleon
structure functions
{F}_{1},\dots,{F}_{5}
in Eq. (3.17) of Ref.
[
73
]
. This is a main conclusion of our work: TMCs for unpolarized protons and neutrons are the same for unpolarized nuclei.

Using Table
6.1
,
the TMCs to the
\tilde{F}_{+}^{A}
,
\tilde{F}_{-}^{A}
, and
\tilde{F}_{0}^{A}

structure functions in the helicity basis are explicitly

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{\pm}^{A}

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}\ \mp\ r_{A}\tilde{F}_{3}^{A}

(A.166)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{\xi_{A}r_{A}}\ \left[\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(\xi_{A})\ \mp\ \tilde{F}_{3}^{A}(\xi_{A})\right]_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \left[\tilde{h}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ \mp\ 2\tilde{h}_{3}(\xi_{A})\right]\

\displaystyle\qquad+\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ ,

(A.167)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{0}^{A}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{r_{A}^{2}}{2x_{A}}\right)\ \tilde{F}_{2}^{A}-\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}

(A.168)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{A}}{2\xi_{A}^{2}r_{A}}\ \left[\tilde{F}_{2}^{A}(\xi_{A})-2\xi\tilde{F}_{1}^{A}(\xi_{A})\right]_{\rm No~TMCs}\ +\ \left(\frac{2M_{A}^{2}x_{A}^{2}}{Q^{2}r_{A}^{2}}\right)\ \tilde{h}_{2}(\xi_{A})\ +\ \left(\frac{4M_{A}^{4}x_{A}^{3}}{Q^{4}r_{A}^{3}}\right)\ \tilde{g}_{2}(\xi_{A})\

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\ \mathcal{O}(\tau>2)\ .

(A.169)

As noted in Eq. (
6.13
), there is a relative factor of

2x_{A}
between the helicity
\tilde{F}_{0}^{A}
and the longitudinal
\tilde{F}_{L}^{A}

structure functions such that
\tilde{F}_{L}^{A}=2x_{A}\tilde{F}_{0}^{A}
.

Appendix B
Derivation of the Full/Leading TMC Parameterization

In this appendix, we derive our parameterizations of Eq. (
7.9
) by approximating the general form of the structure functions in the TMC region.
First, we assume the structure functions
F^{(0)}_{a}(x)
,
a=1,2,3
, vanish at
x=1
.
This follows from our working assumption that the nPDFs vanish at
x\geq 1

c.f., Sec.
5.5
.

Next, we use a finite difference formula to approximate derivatives of
F^{j,(0)}_{a}(x=\xi)
as

\displaystyle F_{a}^{{}^{\prime}(0)}(u=\xi)

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{\gamma_{a}F_{a}^{(0)}(\xi)}{1-\xi}

(B.1)

\displaystyle F_{a}^{j,(0)}(u=\xi)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{0-F_{a}^{j-1,(0)}(\xi)}{(1-\xi)}=(-1)^{j}\frac{\gamma_{a}F_{a}^{(0)}(\xi)}{(1-\xi)^{j}}\quad.

(B.2)

Here,
\gamma_{a}
is a universal correction factor for the first derivative which we assume to
be independent of
x
and the underlying PDFs, and to have a mild
Q
dependence.
We can then expand
F_{a}^{(0)}(u)
about
u=\xi
as

\displaystyle F_{a}^{(0)}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}F_{a}^{j,(0)}(\xi)(u-\xi)^{j}

(B.3)

\displaystyle\approx F_{a}^{(0)}(\xi)\ \left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}(-1)^{j}\frac{\gamma_{a}}{(1-\xi)^{j}}(u-\xi)^{j}\right)

(B.4)

\displaystyle\equiv F_{a}^{(0)}(\xi)\,K_{a}(u,\xi,\gamma_{a})\quad.

(B.5)

Here,

K_{a}(u,\xi,\gamma_{a})=1+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}(-1)^{j}\frac{\gamma_{a}}{(1-\xi)^{j}}(u-\xi)^{j}\,.

(B.6)

As
K_{a}(u,\xi,\gamma)
is independent of the PDFs, this implies that the ratios

\displaystyle\frac{h_{a}(\xi)}{F_{a}^{(0)}(\xi)}=\int_{\xi}^{1}L_{a}(u)K_{a}(\xi,x,\gamma)du

(B.7)

\displaystyle\frac{g_{2}(\xi)}{F_{2}^{(0)}(\xi)}=\int_{\xi}^{1}\frac{u-\xi}{u^{2}}K_{a}(\xi,x,\gamma)du

(B.8)

are also independent of the underlying PDFs.
Here we have defined
L_{1}(u)=2/u
,
L_{2}(u)=1/u^{2}
, and
L_{3}(u)=1/u
.

Evaluating the explicit expression for
K_{a}(u,\xi,\gamma_{a})
in the above relations, we obtain (
7.9a
), (
7.9b
), and (
7.9d
). To calculate
h_{2}(\xi)/F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)
and
g_{2}(\xi)/F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)
, which are needed to calculate the ratio
F_{1}^{TMC}(x)/F_{1}^{Leading-TMC}
, one can assume an approximate Callan-Gross relation. Deviations from the Callan-Gross relation can be absorbed into the fitted
\gamma_{1}(Q)
. We note that in practice, we calculate the massless structure function using
Eq. (
6.14
), namely :

\displaystyle F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=F_{1}^{TMC-ACOT}(x),

(B.9a)

\displaystyle F_{2}^{(0)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi\,r^{2}}{x}F_{2}^{TMC-ACOT}(x),

(B.9b)

\displaystyle F_{3}^{(0)}(\xi)

\displaystyle=rF_{3}^{TMC-ACOT}(x),

(B.9c)

Assuming Callan-Gross relation at the level of the TMC-ACOT structure functions, implies

F_{2}^{(0)}(\xi)=2\xi r^{2}F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)

(B.10)

Using Eq. (
B.10
), one can easily obtain
h_{2}(\xi)/F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)
and
g_{2}(\xi)/F_{1}^{(0)}(\xi)
from
h_{2}(\xi)/F_{2}^{(0)}(\xi)
and
g_{2}(\xi)/F_{2}^{(0)}(\xi)
, as shown in Eqs. (
7.9c
) and (
7.9e
).

If we perform a single parameter fit and take
\gamma_{a}
as a constant,
we find reasonable agreement with the exact TMC result at the level of
\lesssim 0.75\%
for
Q=1.3
GeV and
Q=2
GeV. We can do even better if we perform a 2-parameter fit and replace
\gamma_{a}
with:

\gamma_{a}\rightarrow\gamma_{a}(Q)=\lambda_{a}\ln(Q)^{\delta_{a}}

(B.11)

Thus, we give a mild
Q
dependence to the parameter
\gamma_{a}
. This 2-parameter fit is superior to the one parameter fit as
the agreement is
\lesssim 0.6\%
for
Q=1.3
GeV, and

\lesssim 0.2\%
for
Q=2
GeV which is a typical kinematic cut for the global PDF fits.
